Hence, by rotation,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}G(\theta_{1}\cdot x,\theta_{2}\cdot x,|x|)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}G((\tilde{\theta}_{1}\cdot\theta_{1})x_{1}+(\theta_{1}\cdot\theta_{2})x_{2},x_{2},|x|)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}G((1-(\theta_{1}\cdot\theta_{2})^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}x_{1}+(\theta_{1}\cdot\theta_{2})x_{2},x_{2},|x|)dx.

From (
3.15
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1-\cos(e\cdot\alpha)}{\left((\alpha\cdot\mathbf{b})^{2}+|\alpha|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}d\alpha=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1-\cos(\alpha_{1})}{\left(((\mathbf{b}\cdot e)\alpha_{1}+(|\mathbf{b}|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{2})^{2}+|\alpha|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}d\alpha

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1-\cos(\alpha_{1})}{\left((1+(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})\alpha_{1}^{2}+(\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})\alpha_{2}^{2}+2(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)(|\mathbf{b}|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}+|\alpha^{\prime}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}d\alpha,

where we denote
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha-\alpha_{1}e_{1}-\alpha_{2}e_{2}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\left(1+(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2}\right)\alpha_{1}^{2}+\left(\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2}\right)\alpha_{2}^{2}+2(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)\left(|\mathbf{b}|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2}}\alpha_{1}^{2}+\left((\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{2}+\frac{(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)(|\mathbf{b}|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\alpha_{1}\right)^{2}.

Using elementary linear transformations, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1-\cos(e\cdot\alpha)}{\left((\alpha\cdot\mathbf{b})^{2}+|\alpha|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}d\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1-\cos(\alpha_{1})}{\left(\frac{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2}}\alpha_{1}^{2}+\alpha_{2}^{2}+|\alpha^{\prime}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}d\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{(\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1-\cos(\alpha_{1})}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}d\alpha=\tilde{c}_{d}^{-1}\frac{(\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot e)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

Lemma 3.3

Let
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})=\frac{i\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi\pm\sqrt{(1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2})|\xi|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi)^{2}}}{1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2}}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}f(x):=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})\hat{f}(\xi)\right)(x)=\frac{\mathbf{b}\cdot\nabla f(x)}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\pm\tilde{c}_{d}\mathrm{P.V.}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}},

(3.16)

where
\tilde{c}_{d}
is the positive constant in (
3.14
).

Proof.
We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})\hat{f}(\xi)\right)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})\hat{f}(\xi)\exp\left(ix\cdot\xi\right)d\xi

(3.17)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{b}\cdot\nabla f(x)}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\pm\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\sqrt{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}|\xi|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi)^{2}}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\hat{f}(\xi)\exp\left(ix\cdot\xi\right)d\xi.

By Lemma
3.2
, with
e=\hat{\xi}
, we obtain

\frac{\sqrt{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}|\xi|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi)^{2}}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}=\tilde{c}_{d}|\xi|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\xi}\cdot\alpha)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}.

Equivalently, since the imaginary part is odd in
\alpha
,

\frac{\sqrt{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}|\xi|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi)^{2}}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}=\tilde{c}_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1-e^{-i\xi\cdot\alpha}}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}},

in the principal value sense.
This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\sqrt{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}|\xi|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi)^{2}}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\hat{f}(\xi)\exp(ix\cdot\xi)d\xi=\tilde{c}_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}.

Combining this with (
3.17
) yields (
3.16
).
This completes the proof of the lemma.

Applying Lemma
3.3
to obtain that

\displaystyle\lim_{z\to 0^{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{z}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x-y,z)\mathcal{G}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda^{-}(\xi,\mathbf{b})\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\xi)\exp\left(ix\cdot\xi\right)d\xi

(3.18)

\displaystyle=\tilde{c}_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}\mathcal{G}(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}+\frac{\mathbf{b}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{G}(x)}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}.

Here
\tilde{c}_{d}
is a constant depends only on dimension
d
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\partial_{z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\right)(x,0)}{\mu_{+}}=\frac{1}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\left(\tilde{c}_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}\mathcal{G}(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}+\frac{\mathbf{b}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{G}(x)}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right).

(3.19)

For simplicity, denote

\displaystyle B(\mathbf{b})=\frac{\mathrm{Id}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle}-\frac{\mathbf{b}\otimes\mathbf{b}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{3}},\ \ \ \forall\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(3.20)

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{3}}\mathrm{Id}\leq B(\mathbf{b})\leq\frac{1}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle}\mathrm{Id}.

(3.21)

And

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[f]=-\operatorname{div}\left(\frac{\nabla f}{\langle\nabla f\rangle}\right)=-B(\nabla f):\nabla^{2}f=-\frac{\Delta f}{\langle\nabla f\rangle}+\frac{(\nabla f)^{\top}\nabla^{2}f\nabla f}{\langle\nabla f\rangle^{3}}.

(3.22)

We add subscript in order to emphasize the dependence of the solution to elliptic systems on data. More precisely, we denote
v_{h}^{\pm}(x,z)
the solution to system (
3.3
) with data
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}[h]
,
for any
h:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
.
With a slight abuse of notation, we simply denote

\displaystyle\tilde{v}^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta](x,z)=c^{\pm}{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathbf{b}}[\mathcal{G}[\eta]](x,z).

(3.23)

In the evolution equation (
3.5
), we approximate
(\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}_{\eta})(x,0)
by

\left.(\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta])(x,0)\right|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla\eta(x)}.

This is the central step in the freezing-coefficient argument.

We denote

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{b},\alpha)=\frac{\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{b})}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}},\quad\quad\quad\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{b})=\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}B(\mathbf{b}).

(3.24)

Define

\mathsf{H}^{im}[\eta](x)=-\frac{1}{\mu_{+}}e_{d+1}\cdot\left.\left(\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta(x))\left((\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}_{\eta})(x,z)-(\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta])(x,z)\big|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla\eta(x)}\right)\right)\right|_{z=0}.

(3.25)

The following lemma gives the desired decomposition of the interface equation into an explicit parabolic principal part and a remainder coming from the transmission error.

Lemma 3.4
(Frozen-coefficient contour formulation)

The equation (
1.2
) can be rewritten as the following form:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta(x)-\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{B(\nabla\eta(x)):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}\eta(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}B(\nabla\eta)(x):\nabla^{2}\eta(x-\alpha)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}+\mathsf{H}^{im}[\eta](x),

(3.26)

where
\tilde{c}_{d}=\pi^{-\frac{d+1}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d+1}{2}\right)>0
.

Proof.
For any fixed
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
the evolution equation (
3.5
) can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta(x)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mu_{+}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta(x))(\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta])(x,z))|_{z=0}

(3.27)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mu_{+}}e_{d+1}\cdot\left.\left(\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta(x))\big(\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}_{\eta}(x,z)-\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta](x,z)\big)\right)\right|_{z=0}.

Note that here the second term contributes only as a lower-order remainder term.

By (
3.12
), (
3.19
), the first term can be written as

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mu_{+}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta(x))(\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta])(x,z))|_{z=0}

(3.28)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu_{+}}\left.\left(\nabla\eta(x)\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{v}^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta](x,z)-\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}\partial_{z}\tilde{v}^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta](x,z)\right)\right|_{z=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\left(-\tilde{c}_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}\mathcal{G}[\eta](x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}+\left(\frac{\nabla\eta(x)}{\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{b}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)\cdot\nabla\mathcal{G}[\eta](x)\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{b},\alpha):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}\eta(x)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{(\mathbf{B}(\nabla\eta(x))-\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{b})):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}\eta(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}B(\nabla\eta):\nabla^{2}\eta(x-\alpha)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}+\frac{\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\left(\frac{\nabla\eta(x)}{\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{b}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)\cdot\nabla\mathcal{G}[\eta](x),

with
\mathcal{G},\mathsf{A},\mathbf{B}
defined in (
3.22
) and (
3.24
). We now isolate the principal part of the evolution equation. It is the first term on the right-hand side of (
3.28
), evaluated at
\mathbf{b}=\nabla\eta(x)
. We therefore obtain

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mu_{+}}e_{d+1}

\displaystyle\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta(x))(\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta])(x,z))\big|_{z=0,\mathbf{b}=\nabla\eta(x)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{A}(\nabla\eta(x),\alpha):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}\eta(x)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}+\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}B(\nabla\eta):\nabla^{2}\eta(x-\alpha)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}.

Combining this with (
3.27
) yields (
3.4
) and completes the proof of the lemma.

We now use (
3.4
) to put the interface equation into the form of the
abstract parabolic equation studied in Section
2
.
Assume
\phi
is a smooth function that will be fixed later. Then (
3.27
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta(x)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}\eta(x)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}=\mathsf{H}[\eta](x),

(3.29)

where

\mathsf{H}[\eta]=\mathsf{H}^{ex}[\eta]+\mathsf{H}^{im}[\eta].

Here
\mathsf{H}^{im}
is defined in (
3.25
), and the explicit error is

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}^{ex}[\eta](x)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla\eta(x),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha)):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}\eta(x)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

(3.30)

\displaystyle+\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}\langle\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{2}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}B(\nabla\eta):\nabla^{2}\eta(x-\alpha)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla\eta(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}.

Thus the left-hand side of (
3.29
) is a frozen-coefficient third-order
parabolic operator, while
\mathsf{H}^{ex}
and
\mathsf{H}^{im}
collect respectively the
explicit commutator error and the implicit transmission error. This is precisely
the form to which the Schauder estimates of Section
2
will be applied.

Denote

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[\eta]=v^{\pm}_{\eta}-\tilde{v}^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta],\quad\quad\mathcal{Q}[\eta](x)=(\nabla_{x,z}\omega^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[\eta])(x,z)\big|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla\eta(x),z=0}.

(3.31)

Then

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}^{im}[\eta](x)=-\frac{1}{\mu_{+}}e_{d+1}\cdot\left(\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta(x))\mathcal{Q}[\eta](x)\right).

(3.32)

Moreover, from (
3.3
) and (
3.6
), we know that

\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}=\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[\eta]
solves the elliptic system

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{x,z}(\mathsf{M}(\mathbf{b})\nabla_{x,z}\omega^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}})=\operatorname{div}F_{1,\mathbf{b}}^{\pm},\ \ \text{in}\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\eta}^{\pm},

(3.33)

\displaystyle\omega^{-}_{\mathbf{b}}-\omega^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}=F_{2},\quad\text{ on }\{z=0\},

\displaystyle e_{d+1}\cdot((\mu_{+}^{-1}(\mathsf{M}(\mathbf{b})\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{+}-F_{1,\mathbf{b}}^{+})-\mu_{-}^{-1}(\mathsf{M}(\mathbf{b})\nabla_{x,z}\omega^{-}_{\mathbf{b}}-F_{1,\mathbf{b}}^{-})))=0,\quad\text{ on }\{z=0\},

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}^{\pm}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta)\nabla_{x,z}\omega^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}})=F_{3,\mathbf{b}}^{\pm},\ \ \quad\quad\quad\quad\ \text{on}\ \tilde{\Gamma}_{\eta}^{\pm},

where
\tilde{\Omega}_{\eta}^{\pm},\tilde{\Gamma}_{\eta}^{\pm}
are defined in (
3.4
), and

\displaystyle F_{1,\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}=\left(\mathsf{M}(\mathbf{b})-\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta)\right)\nabla_{x,z}v^{\pm}_{\eta},

(3.34)

\displaystyle F_{2}=\varrho_{0}\eta,

\displaystyle F_{3,\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}=\left.\tilde{\nu}^{\pm}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla\eta)(\nabla_{x,z}v^{\pm}_{\eta}-\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[\eta]))\right|_{\tilde{\Gamma}_{\eta}^{\pm}}.

Remark 3.5

We note that the operator

-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}\eta(x)\,\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

can be written as a pseudo-differential operator of the form (
2.6
). Indeed,

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}\eta(x)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}=(2\pi)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}(x,\xi)\hat{\eta}(\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}d\xi,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{A}}(x,\xi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha):(\xi\otimes\xi)(1-e^{-i\alpha\cdot\xi})\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha):(\xi\otimes\xi)(2-e^{i\alpha\cdot\xi}-e^{-i\alpha\cdot\xi})\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}.

In the second identity we used the symmetry

\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{b},\alpha)=\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{b},-\alpha).

By the ellipticity property (
3.21
), it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}}{(\mu_{+}+\mu_{-})\langle\nabla\phi(x)\rangle^{d+2}}\mathrm{Id}\leq\frac{\tilde{\mathsf{A}}(x,\xi)}{|\xi|^{3}}\leq\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}}{(\mu_{+}+\mu_{-})\langle\nabla\phi(x)\rangle}\mathrm{Id},

\displaystyle|\nabla_{x}^{n_{1}}\nabla_{\xi}^{n_{2}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}(x,\xi)|\lesssim(1+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{n_{1}}})^{n_{1}+10}|\xi|^{3-n_{2}},\ \forall n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}.

Therefore, the operator on the left-hand side of (
3.29
) satisfies condition (
2.7
) with

s=3,\qquad c_{0}=\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}}{(\mu_{+}+\mu_{-})(1+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{\infty}})^{d+2}},\qquad c_{1}=\bigl(1+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{d+m+4}}\bigr)^{d+m+10}.

4
Estimates of nonlinear terms

In this section, we estimate the nonlinear terms appearing on the right-hand side of the contour dynamics formulation (
3.29
). The goal is to show that the error terms
\mathsf{H}^{ex}
and
\mathsf{H}^{im}
satisfy the regularity assumptions required by the Schauder theory developed in Section
2
. In particular, we establish estimates that are compatible with the logarithmically critical topology and exhibit the smallness needed to close the nonlinear argument for sufficiently small times.

The analysis naturally splits into two parts. The term
\mathsf{H}^{ex}
is explicit and arises from the freezing-coefficient decomposition of the principal operator. Its estimate relies on commutator-type bounds and the logarithmic regularity of the interface. The term
\mathsf{H}^{im}
is more delicate, since it depends implicitly on the solution of the elliptic transmission problem. Its control requires the endpoint Schauder estimates for transmission systems established in the previous sections.

To measure the regularity of the interface and the nonlinear terms, we introduce the following norms.
For
a\in(0,1),k\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{m+a}}:=\sup_{\alpha}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m}f\|_{L^{2}}}{|\alpha|^{a}}.

Note that
\|f\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{m+a}}
coincide with the homogeneous Besov norm
\dot{B}^{m+a}_{2,\infty}
.
We fix regularity parameters

\displaystyle\varkappa>1,\ \ \ m\in\mathbb{N},\ \ \ \kappa\in(2,3)\ \text{such that}\ 0<3-\kappa\ll 1.

(4.1)

For
h:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
with
T\in(0,\frac{1}{2}],
define

\displaystyle\|h\|_{T}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|\nabla h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\log^{\varkappa}}\cap L^{2}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}(|\log t|^{\varkappa}\|\nabla h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}+\|\nabla h(t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{m+\kappa}})\right),

(4.2)

\displaystyle\|h\|_{X_{T}}:=\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}}+\|h\|_{T}.

We also define the following non-endpoint norm.

\displaystyle\|h\|_{T,*}:=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\log t|^{\varkappa}(t^{\frac{1}{15}}\|\nabla h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\frac{1}{5}}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}\|\nabla h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})

(4.3)

\displaystyle+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(t^{\frac{1}{15}}\|\nabla h(t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\frac{1}{5}}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}\|\nabla h(t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{m+\kappa}})+T^{\frac{1}{15}}\|h\|_{T}.

For a function
\phi
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we can define
\|\phi\|_{T},\|\phi\|_{X_{T}}
by considering it as a function defined on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and is invariant with respect to the time variable.
Note that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T,*}\lesssim\|f-\phi\|_{T}+\|\phi\|_{T,*}\lesssim\|f-\phi\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{m+3}\cap H^{m+3}}.

(4.4)

The norm
\|\cdot\|_{T,*}
is a non-endpoint auxiliary norm used to quantify the smallness of the nonlinear terms. In particular, (
4.4
) shows that
\|f\|_{T,*}
is small whenever
f
remains close to a smooth reference profile
\phi
in the
\|\cdot\|_{T}
topology and the time interval is sufficiently short.

We first record several elementary composition estimates that will be used repeatedly in the nonlinear estimates below.

Lemma 4.1

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Consider
g,g_{1},g_{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, and
h:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{m+2}\|h^{(k)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1.

Then

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{m}(h\circ g)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m}+\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{m}(h\circ g)\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{m+\alpha}{\alpha}}+\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{m}(h\circ g_{1}-h\circ g_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{m}\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n}}(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m-n}+\mathbf{1}_{m>n}\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}}),

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{m}(h\circ g_{1}-h\circ g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{m}\left\{\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\alpha}}(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m-n}+\mathbf{1}_{m>n}\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad+\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n}}(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{m-n+\alpha}{\alpha}}+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n+\alpha}})\right\}.

Proof.
We only indicate the standard argument. By the Faà di Bruno formula, for any multi-index

\beta
with
|\beta|=m
,

\partial^{\beta}(h\circ g)=\sum_{1\leq q\leq m}h^{(q)}(g)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{q}=\beta\\
|\beta_{i}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}C_{\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}}\prod_{i=1}^{q}\partial^{\beta_{i}}g.

Since the derivatives of
h
are bounded, it follows that

\|\nabla^{m}(h\circ g)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{q=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+\cdots+k_{q}=m\\
k_{i}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{q}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{k_{i}}}.

Using the elementary interpolation inequality

\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{k}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{\frac{m-k}{m-1}}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m}}^{\frac{k-1}{m-1}},\qquad 1\leq k\leq m,

and Young’s inequality, each product is bounded by

C\bigl(\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m}+\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m}}\bigr),

which gives the first estimate.
For the Hölder estimate, applying the
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
seminorm to the same expansion and using

[f_{1}\cdots f_{q}]_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{q}[f_{j}]_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\prod_{i\neq j}\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}},

one obtains

\|\nabla^{m}(h\circ g)\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\sum\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{k_{j}+\alpha}}\prod_{i\neq j}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{k_{i}}},\qquad k_{1}+\cdots+k_{q}=m,k_{i}\in\mathbb{N}.

By interpolation between
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
and
\dot{C}^{m+\alpha}
, namely

\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{k}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{m+\alpha-k}{m}}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\alpha}}^{\frac{k-\alpha}{m}},\qquad\alpha\leq k\leq m+\alpha,

and Young’s inequality, the right-hand side is bounded by

C\bigl(\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{m+\alpha}{\alpha}}+\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\alpha}}\bigr).

This proves the second estimate.

We write

h(g_{1})-h(g_{2})=(g_{1}-g_{2})H,\qquad H(x):=\int_{0}^{1}h^{\prime}\bigl(g_{2}(x)+\theta(g_{1}(x)-g_{2}(x))\bigr)\,d\theta.

Then, by Leibniz’ rule, for every multi-index
\beta
with
|\beta|=m
,

\partial^{\beta}\bigl(h(g_{1})-h(g_{2})\bigr)=\sum_{\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta}C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\partial^{\beta_{1}}(g_{1}-g_{2})\,\partial^{\beta_{2}}H.

Equivalently, in schematic notation,

\nabla^{m}\bigl(h(g_{1})-h(g_{2})\bigr)=\sum_{n=0}^{m}\nabla^{n}(g_{1}-g_{2})\,\nabla^{m-n}H,

where the products stand for finite sums of contractions of derivatives of the indicated orders.
The first two estimates already proved, applied to
H
, imply

\|\nabla^{m-n}H\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m-n}+\mathbf{1}_{m>n}\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}},

and

\|\nabla^{m-n}H\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{m-n+\alpha}{\alpha}}+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n+\alpha}}.

Here, in the case
m=n
, the first estimate is understood by using the trivial bound

\|H\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|h^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1=\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{0}.

Thus the term involving
\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}}
is needed only when
m>n
.

Combining these bounds with the product estimate in
L^{\infty}
gives

\|\nabla^{m}(h\circ g_{1}-h\circ g_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{m}\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n}}\Bigl(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m-n}+\mathbf{1}_{m>n}\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}}\Bigr),

while the Hölder product estimate gives

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{m}(h\circ g_{1}-h\circ g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{m}

\displaystyle\Bigl[\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\alpha}}\Bigl(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m-n}+\mathbf{1}_{m>n}\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n}}\Bigl(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{m-n+\alpha}{\alpha}}+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n+\alpha}}\Bigr)\Bigr].

This completes the proof of the lemma.

We shall also use the following elementary estimates for second-order finite differences.

Lemma 4.2

Denote
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g(x)=\frac{\delta_{\alpha}\delta_{-\alpha}g(x)}{|\alpha|}
. Then for any
\nu\in(0,1)
,
\varkappa>1
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim\min\left\{\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1+\nu}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1-\nu}}^{\frac{1}{2}},\|g\|_{C^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}\right\},

(4.5)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{2}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{2,1}}.

Moreover, for any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim|\beta|^{a}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1+a}},\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{2}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim|\beta|^{a}\|g\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{1+a}}.

(4.6)

Proof.
The first inequality in (
4.5
) follows from the fact that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g(x)|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\alpha}{|\alpha|}\cdot\left(\nabla g(x+\lambda\alpha)-\nabla g(x-\lambda\alpha)\right)d\lambda\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left\{|\alpha|^{\nu}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1+\nu}},\min\{\log^{-\varkappa}(2+|\alpha|^{-1}),|\alpha|^{-1}\}\|g\|_{C^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}\right\},

\displaystyle|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{-1}\left(\left|\delta_{\alpha}g(x)\right|+\left|\delta_{-\alpha}g(x)\right|\right)\lesssim|\alpha|^{-\nu}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1-\nu}}.

The second inequality in (
4.5
) comes from Lemma 2.37 of
[
11
]
.

Then we consider (
4.6
). Note that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim\int_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}+\int_{|\alpha|\geq|\beta|}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|}\|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}+|\beta|\int_{|\alpha|\geq|\beta|}\frac{\|\delta_{\beta}\delta_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\beta||\alpha|^{a}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1-a}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1+a}}\int_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|}|\alpha|^{-d+a}d\alpha+|\beta|\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1+a}}\int_{|\alpha|\geq|\beta|}|\alpha|^{-d+a-1}d\alpha

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|\beta|^{a}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1+a}}.

The second inequality in (
4.6
) can be proved similarly by changing the
L^{\infty}
norm to
L^{2}
in the estimate above. Precisely,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{2}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim\int_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{2}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}+\int_{|\alpha|\geq|\beta|}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{2}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|}\left\|\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\alpha}{|\alpha|}\cdot\delta_{\alpha}\nabla g(x-\lambda\alpha)\right)d\lambda\right\|_{L^{2}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}+|\beta|\int_{|\alpha|\geq|\beta|}\frac{\|\delta_{\beta}\delta_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{2}}}{|\beta||\alpha|^{a}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1-a}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{1+a}}\int_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|}|\alpha|^{-d+a}d\alpha+|\beta|\|g\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{1+a}}\int_{|\alpha|\geq|\beta|}|\alpha|^{-d+a-1}d\alpha

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|\beta|^{a}\|g\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{1+a}}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

4.1
Estimates of
\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f]

We first estimate the explicit critical term
\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f]
, which arises from the freezing-coefficient decomposition of the principal surface-tension operator. Although explicit, this term has the same critical scaling as the leading operator and must be estimated sharply in the logarithmically corrected topology.

We prove the following estimate.

Lemma 4.3

Let
\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f]
be defined by (
3.30
), and let the norm

\|\cdot\|_{T}
be defined by (
4.2
), with

\varkappa,m,\kappa
fixed as in (
4.1
). Then, for every

T\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}

\displaystyle|\log t|^{\varkappa}(t^{\frac{\kappa}{3}}\|\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}\|\nabla^{m}\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim(\|f-\phi\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}\mathfrak{M}_{\phi})^{2}(1+\|(f,\phi)\|_{T})^{2m+5},

(4.7)

where
\mathfrak{M}_{\phi}=\|\phi\|_{H^{m+4}\cap C^{m+4}}
.

Proof.
For brevity, we omit the harmless constant

\frac{\tilde{c}_{d}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}
in the definition of
\mathsf{H}^{ex}
. We decompose

\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f]=\mathcal{P}[f]+\mathcal{W}[f],

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}[f](x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla f(x),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha)):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}f(x)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}[f](x)

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla f(x)\rangle^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}B(\nabla f)(x):\nabla^{2}f(x-\alpha)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla f(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}.

The term
\mathcal{P}[f]
measures the error produced by freezing the coefficient
at
\nabla\phi
, while
\mathcal{W}[f]
is the explicit critical nonlinear term
coming from the variation of the curvature coefficient.

Set

a=\kappa-2\in(0,1).

It is enough to prove that, for every
0\leq n\leq m
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{n+\kappa}{3}}|\log t|^{\varkappa}\|\nabla^{n}\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{a}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}\lesssim(\|f-\phi\|_{T}+\|f\|_{T,*})\|f\|_{T,*}(1+\|(f,\phi)\|_{T})^{2m+5}.

(4.8)

Indeed, the desired estimate (
4.7
) follows from (
4.8
) together
with (
4.4
).

Now we start to prove (
4.8
).

Step 1:
Estimate
\mathcal{P}[f]
. By the symmetry

\mathsf{A}(\cdot,\alpha)=\mathsf{A}(\cdot,-\alpha)
, the first-order difference cancels and
we may rewrite
\mathcal{P}[f]
in terms of the second-order difference operator

\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}[f](x)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla f(x),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha)):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}f(x)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla f(x),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha)):\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}f(x)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}},

where
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g(x)
is defined in Lemma
4.2
.

Applying
\delta_{\beta}\nabla^{n}
, with
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
0\leq n\leq m
, we split the resulting terms according to whether the finite difference
\delta_{\beta}
falls on the second-order difference of
f
or on the coefficient. This gives

\displaystyle|\delta_{\beta}\nabla^{n}\mathcal{P}[f](x)|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\nabla^{n_{1}}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla f(x),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha)):\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\nabla^{2+n-n_{1}}f(x)\right|\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\beta}\nabla^{n_{1}}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla f(\cdot),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(\cdot),\alpha))(x)\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\nabla^{2+n-n_{1}}f(x-\beta)|\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{1}+\mathrm{I}_{2}.

We first consider
\mathrm{I}_{1}
.
We have

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{I}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{n}\|\nabla^{n_{1}}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla f(x),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha))\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,\alpha}\cap L^{\infty}_{\alpha}L^{2}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\nabla^{2+n-n_{1}}f(x)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}.

It follows from Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla f(x),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(x),\alpha))\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,\alpha}\cap L^{\infty}_{\alpha}L^{2}_{x}}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=0}^{k}\|\nabla(f-\phi)\|_{\dot{C}^{l}\cap\dot{\dot{\mathbf{H}}}^{l}}(\|(\nabla f,\nabla\phi)\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{k-l}+\|(\nabla f,\nabla\phi)\|_{\dot{C}^{k-l}})

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{k}{3}}\|f-\phi\|_{T}(1+\|(f,\phi)\|_{T})^{k},\ \ \ \ \forall\ 0\leq k\leq m+2.

Moreover, from Lemma
4.2
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\delta_{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\nabla^{2+n-n_{1}}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim|\beta|^{a}|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+n-n_{1}+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}.

Hence, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{I}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle|\beta|^{a}|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+n+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}\|f-\phi\|_{T}(1+\|(f,\phi)\|_{T})^{n}.

(4.10)

For
\mathrm{I_{2}}
, from (
4.9
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\delta_{\beta}\nabla^{n_{1}}(\mathsf{A}(\nabla f(\cdot),\alpha)-\mathsf{A}(\nabla\phi(\cdot),\alpha))(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\beta|^{a}|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{n_{1}+a}{3}}\|f-\phi\|_{T}(1+\|(f,\phi)\|_{T})^{n_{1}+1}.

Applying Lemma
4.2
again, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\nabla^{2+n-n_{1}}f\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{2+n-n_{1}}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}.

Hence

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{I}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\beta|^{a}|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+n+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}\|f-\phi\|_{T}(1+\|(f,\phi)\|_{T})^{n+1}.

Combining this with (
4.10
), we have

\displaystyle\|\delta_{\beta}\nabla^{n}\mathcal{P}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\lesssim|\beta|^{a}|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+n+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(1+\|(f,\phi)\|_{T})^{n+1}.

(4.11)

We now turn to
\mathcal{W}[f]
. This term has critical order, but it contains
an additional finite difference of
B(\nabla f)
, which provides the gain needed
to close the estimate.

Step 2:
Estimate
\mathcal{W}[f]
.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}[f](x)

\displaystyle={\langle\nabla f(x)\rangle^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}B(\nabla f(x)):\nabla^{2}f(x-\alpha)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla f(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

\displaystyle={\langle\nabla f(x)\rangle^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}B(\nabla f(x)):\nabla^{2}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla f(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}-{\langle\nabla f(x)\rangle^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}B(\nabla f(x)):\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla f(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{W}_{1}[f](x)+\mathcal{W}_{2}[f](x).

We claim that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\leq m+1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\log t|^{\varkappa}t^{\frac{k+2}{3}}\|\nabla^{k}\mathcal{W}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{T,*}^{2}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{2k+5}.

(4.12)

By the symmetry
\mathsf{A}(\cdot,\alpha)=\mathsf{A}(\cdot,-\alpha)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{1}[f](x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\langle\nabla f(x)\rangle^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}B(\nabla f(x)):\nabla^{2}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla f(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}.

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\leq m+1
, by Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}\mathcal{W}_{1}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{j=0}^{k-l}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\nabla^{l}B(\nabla f)\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\sup_{\alpha^{\prime}}\left\|\nabla^{k-l-j}\left(\frac{\langle\nabla f\rangle^{2}}{\langle\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla f(x)\rangle^{d+1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2+j}f\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=0}^{k}t^{-\frac{k-l+2}{3}}\|B(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{C}^{l+\frac{1}{2}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|B(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{C}^{l+\frac{3}{2}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{T,*}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{k-l+2}.

From Lemma
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|B(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{C}^{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2j+1}+\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{j+\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{j}{3}-\frac{1}{6}}\|f\|_{T,*}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{2j+1}.

This implies

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}\mathcal{W}_{1}[f](t)\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{k+2}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}^{2}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{2k+5}.

Similarly, for
\mathcal{W}_{2}[f]
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}\mathcal{W}_{2}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{j=0}^{l}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{j}B(\nabla f)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{2+l-j}f\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}\sup_{\alpha}\left\|\nabla^{k-l}\left(\frac{\langle\nabla f\rangle^{2}}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla f\rangle^{d+1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{k+2}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}^{2}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{2k+5}.

Thus, we obtain (
4.12
).
Then by interpolation, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\log t|^{\varkappa}t^{\frac{n+\kappa}{3}}\|\delta_{\beta}\nabla^{n}\mathcal{W}[f](t)\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\lesssim|\beta|^{a}\|f\|_{T,*}^{2}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{2n+5},\ \ \ \forall n\in\mathbb{N},n\leq m.

Combining this with (
4.11
) yields (
4.8
).
This completes the proof of the lemma.

4.2
Estimates of
\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]

We now estimate the implicit term
\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]
defined in (
3.32
). This is the most delicate part of the nonlinear analysis, since
\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]
depends on the correction
\omega^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}}[f]
, which solves the elliptic transmission problem (
3.33
). Unlike
\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f]
, this term is not given by an explicit singular integral of the interface alone.

Throughout this subsection, we assume that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{T}}<\infty,\qquad\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\operatorname{dist}(f,\Gamma^{\pm})\geq\mathbf{r}.

(4.13)

Now we start to estimate the Hölder norm of solution to system (
3.33
).
Recalling (
3.31
), we have

\displaystyle v_{f}^{\pm}=\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]+\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f].

(4.14)

The estimation of force terms in (
3.33
) requires estimation of
\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
which is defined in
(
3.23
):

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f](x,z)=c^{\pm}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x-y,z)\mathcal{G}[f](y)dy.

(4.15)

As a preparation, we first give some estimates of
\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
.

We begin with an explicit representation of the kernel
K_{\mathbf{b}}
defined in (
3.11
).

Lemma 4.4

Let
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})=\frac{i\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi\pm\sqrt{(1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2})|\xi|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi)^{2}}}{1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2}},

K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(ix\cdot\xi+\lambda^{-}(\xi,\mathbf{b})z\right)d\xi,&z\geq 0,\\
\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(ix\cdot\xi+\lambda^{+}(\xi,\mathbf{b})z\right)d\xi,&z<0.\end{cases}

Then

K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)=c_{d}\frac{|z|}{\left((x\cdot\mathbf{b}+z)^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}},\ \ \ \forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ \ z\neq 0,

(4.16)

where the constant
c_{d}=\frac{\Gamma(\frac{d+1}{2})}{\pi^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}
ensures that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,1)dx=1
. Moreover, when
z=0
, there holds
K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,0)=\delta(x)
.

Proof.
The case
z=0
follows from the definition of the Fourier kernel, and the case

\mathbf{b}=0
reduces to the classical Poisson kernel. We therefore assume

z\neq 0
and
\mathbf{b}\neq 0
. We treat the case
z<0
; the case
z>0
is identical,
with
\lambda^{-}
replaced by
\lambda^{+}
. We first recall the explicit formula of Poisson kernel (see
[
52
, Chapter III, section 2.1]
)

\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(ix\cdot\xi+|\xi|z\right)d\xi=c_{d}\frac{-z}{(|x|^{2}+|z|^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}},~z<0.

(4.17)

1) Case
d=1
, we have,

\displaystyle K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(ix\cdot\xi+\frac{i\mathbf{b}\xi+|\xi|}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}z\right)d\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(i\left(x+\frac{\mathbf{b}z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)\cdot\xi+|\xi|\frac{z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)d\xi.

Applying (
4.17
) with
d=1
to get

\displaystyle K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)

\displaystyle=c_{1}\frac{-\frac{z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}}{\left(x+\frac{\mathbf{b}z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)^{2}}=c_{1}\frac{-z}{\left(x\cdot\mathbf{b}+z\right)^{2}+|x|^{2}}.

2) Case
d\geq 2
.
Using the transformation (
3.15
), we obtain

\displaystyle K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(ix\cdot\xi+\frac{i\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi+\sqrt{(1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2})|\xi|^{2}-(\mathbf{b}\cdot\xi)^{2}}}{1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2}}z\right)d\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(i|x|\left((1-(\frac{x\cdot\mathbf{b}}{|x||\mathbf{b}|})^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{1}+\frac{x\cdot\mathbf{b}}{|x||\mathbf{b}|}\xi_{2}\right)+\frac{i|\mathbf{b}|\xi_{2}+\sqrt{(1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2})|\xi|^{2}-|\mathbf{b}|^{2}\xi_{2}^{2}}}{1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2}}z\right)d\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(i\frac{((|x||\mathbf{b}|)^{2}-(x\cdot\mathbf{b})^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|\mathbf{b}|}\xi_{1}+i\left(\frac{x\cdot\mathbf{b}}{|\mathbf{b}|}+\frac{|\mathbf{b}|z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)\xi_{2}+\frac{\sqrt{(1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2})|\xi|^{2}-|\mathbf{b}|^{2}\xi_{2}^{2}}}{1+|\mathbf{b}|^{2}}z\right)d\xi.

By simple changes of variables, we obtain

\displaystyle K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(i\frac{((|x||\mathbf{b}|)^{2}-(x\cdot\mathbf{b})^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|\mathbf{b}|\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle}\xi_{1}+i\left(\frac{x\cdot\mathbf{b}}{|\mathbf{b}|}+\frac{|\mathbf{b}|z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)\xi_{2}+|\xi|\frac{z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)\frac{d\xi}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d-1}}.

Finally, applying (
4.17
) yields

\displaystyle K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{d}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d-1}}\frac{-\frac{z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}}{\left(\frac{(|x||\mathbf{b}|)^{2}-(x\cdot\mathbf{b})^{2}}{|\mathbf{b}|^{2}\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}+\left(\frac{x\cdot\mathbf{b}}{|\mathbf{b}|}+\frac{|\mathbf{b}|z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{d}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d-1}}\frac{-\frac{z}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}}{\left(\frac{(x\cdot\mathbf{b})^{2}+2zx\cdot\mathbf{b}+z^{2}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}+\frac{z^{2}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}=c_{d}\frac{-z}{\left((x\cdot\mathbf{b}+z)^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

Remark 4.5

Since

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)\,dx=1,\qquad z\neq 0,

the family
K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)
forms an approximation of the identity. In
particular,

K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\to\delta_{0}

in the sense of distributions as
z\to 0
.

We next collect several estimates for the frozen Poisson kernel
K_{\mathbf{b}}
. These bounds will be used to control the frozen velocity
\tilde{v}^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}}[f]
and its normal derivative near the interface. The main point is that
x
-derivatives can be estimated directly from the explicit kernel, while
z
-derivatives require the Fourier representation and the operator
\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}
defined in (
3.3
).

Lemma 4.6

We have,

(1) For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla_{x}^{n}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)|\lesssim\frac{|z|\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{n}}{\left((x\cdot\mathbf{b}+z)^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1+n}{2}}},\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},z\neq 0.

(4.18)

(2) For any
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla_{x}^{n_{1}}\partial_{z}^{n_{2}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)|\lesssim{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{1+n_{1}+n_{2}}}{\left(|x|^{2}+\frac{|z|^{2}}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{d+n_{1}+n_{2}}{2}}},\quad\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},z\neq 0.

(4.19)

(3) For any
z\neq 0
,
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)dx=C_{d},

(4.20)

where
C_{d}
is a constant independent of
z
and
\mathbf{b}
.

(4) For any
a\in(0,1)
,
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
z,\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,z)-K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,\beta)||y|^{a}dy\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}|z-\beta|^{a},\ \ \ \forall a\in(0,1).

(4.21)

Proof.
The point-wise estimates (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) follow directly from the formula (
4.16
).

It remains to prove (
4.20
) and (
4.21
). Observing the scaling invariant property

K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)=|z|^{-d}K_{\mathbf{b}}(|z|^{-1}x,|z|^{-1}z),

we obtain that for any
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
z\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)dx=|z|^{-d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(|z|^{-1}x,|z|^{-1}z)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,\pm 1)dx,\ \ \ z\in\mathbb{R}^{\pm}.

By a rotation transformation and changes of variables, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,\pm 1)dx

\displaystyle=c_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\left((x\cdot\mathbf{b}\pm 1)^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}dx=c_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\left((x_{1}|\mathbf{b}|\pm 1)^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}dx

\displaystyle=c_{d}\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\left(|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}dx=c_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle|x|\rangle^{d+1}}dx=C.

This completes the proof of (
4.20
). Finally, for any
z,\beta>0
, by (
4.19
) we have

\displaystyle|K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,z)-K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,\beta)|\lesssim\frac{\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}{|y|^{d}}\min\left\{1,\frac{|z-\beta|}{|y|}\right\}.

This implies (
4.21
). Then we complete the proof of the lemma.

In view of formula (
4.15
), when estimating
\nabla_{x}^{n}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
, one can freely interchange the
x
-derivatives between the kernel
K_{\mathbf{b}}
and the function
\mathcal{G}[f]
. Thus, Lemma
4.6
suffices to bound derivatives of
\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
with respect to
x
to any order. However, in estimating
\nabla_{x}^{n}\partial_{z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
, we encounter the term
\partial_{z}K_{\mathbf{b}}
, which becomes singular near the interface
{z=0}
. Indeed, from (
4.19
) we only obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{z}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)|dx\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{3}|z|^{-1}.

To overcome this difficulty, we further analyze
\partial_{z}K_{\mathbf{b}}
via its Fourier transform. Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\partial_{z}K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z))(\xi)=\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})\exp(\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})z),\ \ \ z\in\mathbb{R}^{\mp}.

Hence we can write

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)=(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast(\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}g)(\cdot))(x),

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}f(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})\hat{f}(\xi)\right)(x).

We first prove the following estimates for

\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}f
.

Lemma 4.7

For any
a\in(0,1)
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}f\|_{\dot{C}^{m+a}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{m+1+a}},

(4.22)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}.

(4.23)

Proof.
Observe that

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}^{m}\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}f(x)=\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(\nabla_{x}^{m}f)(x).

From Lemma
3.3
, to prove (
4.22
) and (
4.23
), it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}\right\|_{\dot{C}^{a}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{1+a}},\quad\quad\quad\sup_{\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}.

(4.24)

Indeed, by symmetry, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\delta_{\alpha}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}f(x)}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{b}\rangle^{d+1}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}},

where
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
is defined in Lemma
4.2
. Then (
4.24
) follows from Lemma
4.2
.

Lemma 4.8

Consider
K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)
defined in (
4.16
). For any fixed
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
m_{1}\in\{0,1\}
,
a\in(0,1)
, there hold
2
2

2

Note that
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{C}^{a}_{x,z}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-})}=\|\cdot\|_{\dot{C}^{a}_{x,z}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+})}+\|\cdot\|_{\dot{C}^{a}_{x,z}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-})}
is different from
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{C}^{a}_{x,z}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1})}
.

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)\right\|_{\dot{C}^{a}_{x,z}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-})}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m_{1}+a}},

(4.25)

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{z}K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-})}\lesssim\|g\|_{C^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}.

(4.26)

Proof.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z))(\xi)=\left(\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})\right)^{m_{1}}\exp(\lambda^{\pm}(\xi,\mathbf{b})z),\ \ \ z\in\mathbb{R}^{\mp}.

Hence we can write

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)=(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast(\mathfrak{L}^{m_{1}}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}g)(\cdot))(x),

where the operator
\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}
is defined in (
3.16
).
By (
4.23
) and (
4.20
), we obtain (
4.26
).

By (
4.22
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}(\mathbb{R})\dot{C}^{a}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{L}^{m_{1}}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}g\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m_{1}+a}}.

(4.27)

Then we estimate the Hölder norm in
z
-variable. For simplicity, we only consider
z\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Then for any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)-\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,\beta)\ast g)(x)

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,z)-K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,\beta))(\mathfrak{L}_{-,\mathbf{b}}^{m_{1}}g)(x-y)dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,z)-K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,\beta))\left((\mathfrak{L}_{-,\mathbf{b}}^{m_{1}}g)(x-y)-(\mathfrak{L}_{-,\mathbf{b}}^{m_{1}}g)(x)\right)dy.

where in the last identity we used (
4.20
) in Lemma
4.6
to get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,z)-K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,\beta)\big)dy=0.

Then, by (
4.21
) and (
4.22
), we obtain that

\displaystyle|((\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}K_{\mathbf{b}})(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)-((\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}K_{\mathbf{b}})(\cdot,\beta)\ast g)(x)|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,z)-K_{\mathbf{b}}(y,\beta)||y|^{a}dy\ \|\mathfrak{L}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{m_{1}}g\|_{\dot{C}^{a}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}|z-\beta|^{a}\ \|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m_{1}+a}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\dot{C}^{a}_{z}(\mathbb{R}^{+}))}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m_{1}+a}}.

Combining this with (
4.27
), we get

\displaystyle\sup_{z>0}\|\partial_{z}^{m_{1}}(K_{\mathbf{b}}(\cdot,z)\ast g)(x)\|_{\dot{C}^{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+})}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m_{1}+a}}.

The estimate in
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-}
follows by the same argument. This completes the proof of the lemma.

We now derive estimates for the frozen velocity
\tilde{v}^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}}[f]
.

Lemma 4.9

For any
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
defined in (
4.15
), and any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds

\displaystyle\sum_{+,-}\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\pm})}\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{C^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}},\ \ \varkappa>1,

(4.28)

\displaystyle\sum_{+,-}\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\pm})}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{1+a}},\ \ \ a\in(0,1),

(4.29)

\displaystyle\sum_{+,-}\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\pm})}\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(4.30)

Proof.
The estimates (
4.28
) and (
4.29
) follow directly from
Lemma
4.8
, applied to
g=\nabla_{x}^{n}\mathcal{G}[f]
.
It remains to prove (
4.30
).
By (
3.9
) and Parseval’s Theorem, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{+}[f]\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\xi|^{2n}(|\lambda^{-}(\xi,\mathbf{b})|+|\xi|)^{2}|e^{\lambda^{-}(\xi,\mathbf{b})z}|^{2}|\hat{\mathcal{G}}[f](\xi)|^{2}d\xi dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\xi|^{2n+1}|\hat{\mathcal{G}}[f](\xi)|^{2}d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

We next need a basic energy estimate for the full transmission solution

v_{f}^{\pm}
. This estimate is subcritical and will be used only to control the

L^{2}
part of the forcing terms in the equation for
\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
.

Lemma 4.10

Let
v^{\pm}=v_{f}^{\pm}
be the weak solution to system (
3.3
), then

\displaystyle\sum_{+,-}\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{\pm})}\lesssim(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{8}\|f\|_{H^{5/2}}.

(4.31)

Proof.
Recall that
v^{\pm}=v^{\pm}_{f}
is the weak solution to the elliptic system

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{x,z}(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{\pm})=0,\ \ \text{in}\ \tilde{\Omega}^{\pm}_{f},

(4.32)

\displaystyle v^{-}-v^{+}=\mathcal{G}[f]+\varrho_{0}f,\quad\text{ on }\{z=0\},

\displaystyle e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)(\mu_{+}^{-1}\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}-\mu_{-}^{-1}\nabla_{x,z}v^{-}))=0,\quad\text{ on }\{z=0\},

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}^{\pm}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{\pm})=0,\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{on}\ \tilde{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{f}.

Using
v^{\pm}
as a test function to (
4.32
), we obtain

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\mu_{+}^{-1}\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+}}\operatorname{div}_{x,z}(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+})v^{+}dxdz+\mu_{-}^{-1}\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{-}}\operatorname{div}_{x,z}(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{-})v^{-}dxdz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mu_{+}^{-1}\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+}}(\nabla_{x,z}v^{+})^{\top}\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}dxdz-\mu_{-}^{-1}\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{-}}(\nabla_{x,z}v^{-})^{\top}\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{-}dxdz

\displaystyle-\mu_{+}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}(x,0))v^{+}(x,0)dx+\mu_{-}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{-}(x,0))v^{-}(x,0)dx.

By the ellipticity of
\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)
, we have

\displaystyle(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{-2}\left(\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{-})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+}}(\nabla_{x,z}v^{+})^{\top}\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}dxdz+\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{-}}(\nabla_{x,z}v^{-})^{\top}\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{-}dxdz.

On the other hand, with the boundary conditions in (
4.32
), we obtain that

\displaystyle-\mu_{+}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}(x,0))v^{+}(x,0)dx+\mu_{-}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{-}(x,0))v^{-}(x,0)dx

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{+}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}(x,0))\left(\mathcal{G}[f]+\varrho_{0}f\right)dx.

By the trace theorem, there exists an extension
\mathbf{f}
in
\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+}

with trace
\mathcal{G}[f]+\varrho_{0}f
on
\{z=0\}
and

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+})}\lesssim\left\|\mathcal{G}[f]+\varrho_{0}f\right\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}},

then applying Green’s formula and (
4.32
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}(x,0))\left(\mathcal{G}[f]+\varrho_{0}f\right)(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+}}\operatorname{div}_{x,z}\left(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}(x,z)\mathbf{f}(x,z)\right)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+}}(\nabla_{x,z}\mathbf{f}(x,z))^{\top}\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}(x,z)dxdz.

Hence, it follows from Hölder’s inequality

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{-})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+||\nabla f||_{L^{\infty}})^{4}\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+})}\|\nabla_{x,z}\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{10}\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+})}^{2}+C(1+||\nabla f||_{L^{\infty}})^{8}\left\|\mathcal{G}[f]+\varrho_{0}f\right\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{+})}+\|\nabla_{x,z}v^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{-})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+||\nabla f||_{L^{\infty}})^{8}(\|\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|f\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}).

Then we obtain (
4.31
) and complete the proof of the lemma.

The next lemma shows that, away from the interface
\{z=0\}
, the frozen velocity contributes only lower-order terms. This follows from the decay of the Poisson kernel in the normal variable.

Lemma 4.11

For any
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
be as defined in (
4.15
). Then for any
\sigma>0
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and any
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\pm}\cap\{|z|\geq\sigma\})}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\pm}\cap\{|z|\geq\sigma\})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\sigma)^{-(n+2)}\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2n+8}(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{5}\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}.

Proof.
It follows from (
4.19
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{|z|\geq\sigma}\|\nabla_{x}^{n_{1}}\partial_{z}^{n_{2}}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)\|_{L_{x}^{1}}\lesssim(1+\sigma)^{-(1+n_{1}+n_{2})}\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{3+2(n_{1}+n_{2})},\ \ \ \forall n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}.

Then by the formula of
\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]
in (
4.15
), one has

\displaystyle\sup_{|z|\geq\sigma}|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]|\lesssim\sup_{|z|\geq\sigma}\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}K_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\|\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+\sigma)^{-(n+2)}\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2n+5}\|\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Moreover,

\|\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{5}\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}.

The Hölder estimate is obtained similarly. Using the
L^{1}
bounds for one
additional derivative of the kernel and interpolation, we get

\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{a}(\{|z|\geq\sigma\})}\lesssim(1+\sigma)^{-(n+2)}\langle\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2n+8}\|\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Combining the last two estimates gives the desired result.

With Lemmas
4.9
and
4.11
available, we now turn to the
elliptic correction
\omega^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}}[f]
and estimate the boundary quantity

\mathcal{Q}[f](x)=\left.(\nabla_{x,z}\omega^{+}_{\mathbf{b}}[f])(x,z)\right|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x),\,z=0}.

This is the key estimate for the implicit nonlinear term
\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]
. The main
difficulty is that
\omega^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}}[f]
is not explicit; it solves the
transmission problem (
3.33
). We combine the endpoint transmission
estimate, the bounds for the frozen velocity, and the separation condition
(
4.13
).

Lemma 4.12

Consider
\|\cdot\|_{X_{T}}
defined in (
4.2
), then for any
T\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, and any function
f
satisfying (
4.13
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathcal{Q}[f](t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(\|f-\phi\|_{X_{T}}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}\mathfrak{M}_{\phi})(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}})^{10},

(4.33)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}(|\log t|^{\varkappa}\|\nabla^{m}\mathcal{Q}[f](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}}+\|\nabla^{m}\mathcal{Q}[f](t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}})\lesssim(\|f-\phi\|_{X_{T}}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}\mathfrak{M}_{\phi})^{2}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}})^{2m+10},

(4.34)

where
\mathfrak{M}_{\phi}=\|\phi\|_{H^{m+4}\cap C^{m+4}}
.

Proof.
We split the proof into two parts. First, we obtain the zeroth-order

L^{\infty}
bound (
4.33
) by applying the endpoint Lipschitz estimate to
the original transmission solution
v_{f}^{\pm}
. This avoids differentiating the
equation for
\omega^{\pm}_{\mathbf{b}}
directly. Second, we prove the critical

\dot{C}^{a}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}
estimate, where
a=\kappa-2
, by applying the frozen
transmission representation to (
3.33
).

We denote

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\tau}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(0,\tau)\ \text{if}\ \tau>0,\ \quad\quad\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\tau}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\tau,0)\ \text{if}\ \tau<0.

We first prove (
4.33
). Note that

\|\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{b}}\|\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{+}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{r/2})},

where the parameter
r<\frac{\mathbf{r}}{10}
will be fixed later.
To avoid dealing with the remainder term and the issue of losing derivatives, we go back to the equation of
v_{f}^{\pm}
rather than directly estimating
\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}
. From (
3.3
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{x,z}(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}v^{\pm})=0,\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-4r}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{4r},

\displaystyle v^{-}-v^{+}=\mathcal{G}[f]+\varrho_{0}f,\quad\text{ on }\{z=0\},

\displaystyle e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\nabla_{x,z}(\mu_{+}^{-1}v^{+}-\mu_{-}^{-1}v^{-}))=0,\quad\text{ on }\{z=0\}.

By Lemma
6.3
, we can take
r
sufficiently small such that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x,z}v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-r/2}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{r/2})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}+\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}+C(r)\|\nabla_{x,z}v\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-3r}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{3r})}.

Combining this with (
4.31
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x,z}v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-r/2}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{r/2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{G}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}+\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\log^{\varkappa}}}+(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{7}\|f\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{2,\log^{\varkappa}}\cap\dot{C}^{\log^{\varkappa}}}+\|f\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}})(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{7}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{2}{3}}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{X_{T}})(1+\|f\|_{T})^{7},

where
\|\cdot\|_{T,*}
is defined in (
4.3
).
Combining this with Lemma
4.9
and the definition
\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}=v^{\pm}-\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}
in (
4.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{b}}\|\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\pm r/2})}\lesssim t^{-\frac{2}{3}}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{X_{T}})(1+\|f\|_{T})^{7},

which yields (
4.33
) as a result of (
4.4
).

Then we prove (
4.34
). For simplicity, we only consider the case

m=0
, namely the estimate of
\|\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{a}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}
.
The general case follows by applying horizontal derivatives
\nabla_{x}^{m}
to
the equation and repeating the same argument.
Note that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{\beta}\frac{\|\delta_{\beta}^{x}(\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{+})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x)}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\beta|^{a}}

(4.36)

\displaystyle+\sup_{\beta}\frac{\|(\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{+})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x)}-(\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{+})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x-\beta)}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\beta|^{a}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{2},

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{\beta}\frac{\|\delta_{\beta}^{x}(\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{+})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x)}\|_{L^{2}}}{|\beta|^{a}}

(4.37)

\displaystyle+\sup_{\beta}\frac{\|(\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{+})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x)}-(\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}^{+})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x-\beta)}\|_{L^{2}}}{|\beta|^{a}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}.

We estimate the right hand side of (
4.36
) term by term.
Applying Lemma
6.1
to (
3.33
) with
A(x)=\mathsf{M}(\mathbf{b})
,
(g,h)=(F_{1,\mathbf{b}},F_{2})
, we obtain for any
(x,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{r}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-r}
,

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathbf{b}}(x,z)=\mathsf{T}_{1,\mathbf{b}}F_{1,\mathbf{b}}(x,z)+\mathsf{T}_{2,\mathbf{b}}F_{2}(x,z)+\mathrm{LO}(\omega_{\mathbf{b}},F_{1,\mathbf{b}})(x,z),

with

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{1,\mathbf{b}}F_{1,\mathbf{b}}(x,z)=-\frac{\mu_{+}\mathbf{1}_{z\in(0,r)}+\mu_{-}\mathbf{1}_{z\in(-r,0)}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\frac{1}{r}\int_{r}^{2r}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\tau}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-\tau}}F_{1,\mathbf{b}}(y,w)\cdot\nabla_{y,w}\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{b}}^{D}(x,z,y,w)dydwd\tau

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\frac{\mu_{+}\mu_{-}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\sum_{+,-}\left(\frac{1}{r\mu_{\pm}}\int_{r}^{2r}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{\pm\tau}}F_{1,\mathbf{b}}(y,w)\cdot\nabla_{y,w}\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{b}}^{N}(x,z,y,w)dydwd\tau\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{2,\mathbf{b}}F_{2}(x,z)=\frac{\mu_{+}\mathbf{1}_{z\in(0,r)}+\mu_{-}\mathbf{1}_{z\in(-r,0)}}{\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e_{d+1}\cdot(\mathsf{M}(\mathbf{b})\nabla_{y,w}\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{b}}^{D}(x,z,y,0)F_{2}(y))dy,

where the kernels
\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{b}}^{N}
,
\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{b}}^{D}
are defined by (
6.10
) with
Q_{x_{0}}
replaced by
\mathsf{M}(\mathbf{b})
. From Lemma
6.5
, we know that the lower order term
\mathrm{LO}(\omega_{\mathbf{b}},F_{1,\mathbf{b}})
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\mathrm{LO}(\omega_{\mathbf{b}},F_{1,\mathbf{b}})(x,0^{+})|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x)}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\sup_{\mathbf{b}}\|\nabla_{\mathbf{b}}^{l}\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\mathrm{LO}(\omega_{\mathbf{b}},F_{1,\mathbf{b}})(x,0^{+})\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim C(r,n)\sup_{\mathbf{b}}(\|\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{2r}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-2r})}+\|F_{1,\mathbf{b}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{2r}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-2r})}),\ \ \forall n\in\mathbb{N},

where
l\geq\frac{d}{2}+1
.
Combining this with Lemma
4.10
yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{n}\nabla_{x,z}\mathrm{LO}(\omega_{\mathbf{b}},F_{1,\mathbf{b}})(x,0^{+})|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x)}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim C(r,n)(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{10}\|f\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}},\ \ \forall n\in\mathbb{N}.

Denote

\mathsf{L}_{1}(x,\mathbf{b})=(\nabla_{x,z}\mathsf{T}_{1,\mathbf{b}}F_{1,\mathbf{b}}(x,z))|_{z=0^{+}},\ \ \ \ \mathsf{L}_{2}(x,\mathbf{b})=(\nabla_{x,z}\mathsf{T}_{2,\mathbf{b}}F_{2}(x,z))|_{z=0^{+}}.

Then it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}[f](x)=\mathsf{L}_{1}(x,\nabla f(x))+\mathsf{L}_{2}(x,\nabla f(x))+R(x),

where
R(x)
satisfies

\displaystyle\|R\|_{\dot{C}^{a}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}\lesssim(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{10}\|f\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{X_{T}})(1+\|f\|_{T})^{10}.

(4.38)

By Lemma
6.2
and (
4.25
) in Lemma
4.8
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{L}_{2}(x,\nabla f(x))\|_{\dot{C}^{a}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{2}\|F_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{1+a}}\lesssim(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{2}\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{a}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}

(4.39)

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{10}.

To control
\mathsf{L}_{1}
, it suffices to consider

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}^{i}_{1}(x,\mathbf{b})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d+1}}K_{i}(x,z,y,w)\tilde{F}_{1,\mathbf{b}}(y,w)dydw\Big|_{z=0^{+}},\ \ i=1,2,

with
\tilde{F}_{1,\mathbf{b}}(y,w)=F_{1,\mathbf{b}}(y,w)\mathbf{1}_{|w|\leq\tau}
for
\tau\in(r,2r)
, and

\displaystyle K_{1}(x,z,y,w)=(\nabla_{x,z}\nabla_{y,w}\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{b}}^{D})(x,z,y,w),

\displaystyle K_{2}(x,z,y,w)=(\nabla_{x,z}\nabla_{y,w}\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{b}}^{N})(x,z,y,w).

By Lemma
6.4
, we obtain for any
p\in\{2,\infty\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\sup_{\beta}\frac{\|\mathsf{L}^{i}_{1}(x,\nabla f(x))-\mathsf{L}^{i}_{1}(x-\beta,\nabla f(x))\|_{L^{p}_{x}}}{|\beta|^{a}}

(4.40)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,u\in\mathbb{R}^{d},z,w\in\mathbb{R}\\
|u|\leq|(y,w)|\end{subarray}}\frac{\|(\tilde{F}_{1,\mathbf{b}}(x,z)-\tilde{F}_{1,\mathbf{b}}(x-y,z-w))|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x+u)}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}}{|(y,w)|^{a}}.

Recalling the definition of
F_{1,\mathbf{b}}
in (
3.34
), we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{F}_{1,\mathbf{b}}(x,z)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x+u)}|+|\tilde{F}_{1,\mathbf{b}}(x-y,z-w)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x+u)}|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq(|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f(x))-\mathsf{M}(\nabla f(x+u))|+|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f(x-y))-\mathsf{M}(\nabla f(x+u))|)\|\nabla_{x,z}v_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{2r}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-2r})}.

Note that

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f(x))-\mathsf{M}(\nabla f(x^{\prime}))\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|x-x^{\prime}|^{a}\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{a}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}}).

By (
4.35
), the right hand side of (
4.40
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}\|\nabla_{x,z}v_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{2r}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-2r})}

\displaystyle(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{X_{T}})(1+\|f\|_{T})^{10},\ \ \ p=\infty,

\displaystyle\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}\|\nabla_{x,z}v_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{2r}\cup\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{-2r})}

\displaystyle(1+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{2+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{X_{T}})(1+\|f\|_{T})^{10},\ \ \ p=2.

Combining this with (
4.38
), (
4.39
), and the definitions (
4.36
), (
4.37
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|\leq r}\frac{\|\mathsf{L}_{1}(x,\nabla f(x))-\mathsf{L}_{1}(x-\beta,\nabla f(x))\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\beta|^{a}}+\|\mathsf{L}_{2}(x,\nabla f(x))\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}+\|R\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}

(4.41)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}}))(1+\|f\|_{T})^{10},

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|\leq r}\frac{\|\mathsf{L}_{1}(x,\nabla f(x))-\mathsf{L}_{1}(x-\beta,\nabla f(x))\|_{L^{2}}}{|\beta|^{a}}+\|\mathsf{L}_{2}(x,\nabla f(x))\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}+\|R\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}

(4.42)

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{2+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}}))(1+\|f\|_{T})^{10}.

Then we estimate
\mathbf{Q}_{2}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}
. Using the property that

\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}}+\omega_{\mathbf{b}}
is independent of
\mathbf{b}
, and applying (
3.12
), (
3.19
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|(\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x)}-(\nabla_{x,z}\omega_{\mathbf{b}})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x-\beta)}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|(\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x)}-(\nabla_{x,z}\tilde{v}_{\mathbf{b}})(x,0)|_{\mathbf{b}=\nabla f(x-\beta)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta_{\alpha}\mathcal{G}(x)\left(\frac{1}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla f(x)\rangle^{d+1}}-\frac{1}{\langle\hat{\alpha}\cdot\nabla f(x-\beta)\rangle^{d+1}}\right)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left|\frac{\nabla f(x)}{\langle\nabla f(x)\rangle^{2}}-\frac{\nabla f(x-\beta)}{\langle\nabla f(x-\beta)\rangle^{2}}\right|\|\nabla\mathcal{G}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

We have

\displaystyle\|I_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\beta|^{\frac{a}{2}}\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{\frac{a}{2}}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\frac{a}{2}}}\int_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|}\|\delta_{\alpha}\delta_{-\alpha}\mathcal{G}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}+|\beta|\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{1}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{1}}\int_{|\alpha|\geq|\beta|}\|\delta_{\alpha}\mathcal{G}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\beta|^{a}(\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{\frac{a}{2}}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\frac{a}{2}}}\|\mathcal{G}\|_{\dot{C}^{1+\frac{a}{2}}}+\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{1}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{1}}\|\mathcal{G}\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}).

And

\displaystyle\|I_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\lesssim|\beta|^{a}\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}^{a}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}\|\nabla\mathcal{G}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Combining this with (
3.22
) yields

\displaystyle\|I_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}+\|I_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\lesssim|\beta|^{a}|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}^{2}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}})^{10},

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{2}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}\lesssim|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}^{2}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}})^{10}.

(4.43)

Then we conclude from (
4.36
), (
4.37
), (
4.41
), (
4.42
) and (
4.43
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}\lesssim|\log t|^{-\varkappa}t^{-\frac{2+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}}))(1+\|f\|_{T})^{10},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{a}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{2+a}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(\|f\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}}))(1+\|f\|_{T})^{10}.

Combining this with (
4.4
), we deduce (
4.34
) and complete the proof of the lemma.

We now translate the estimates for the boundary quantity
\mathcal{Q}[f]
into estimates for the implicit nonlinear term
\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]
.

Lemma 4.13

Let
\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]
be defined by (
3.32
), and let the norm

\|\cdot\|_{X_{T}}
be defined by (
4.2
), with

\varkappa,m,\kappa
fixed as in (
4.1
). Then there exist

\sigma,T>0
, depending only on

\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{m+4}}
,
\|\nabla\underline{b}^{\pm}\|_{C^{1}}
, and the
separation constant
\mathbf{r}
, such that for every

f\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}_{T,\phi}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\log t|^{\varkappa}(t^{\frac{\kappa}{3}}\|\mathsf{H}^{im}[f](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}\|\nabla_{x}^{m}\mathsf{H}^{im}[f](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(t^{\frac{\kappa}{3}}\|\mathsf{H}^{im}[f](t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}\|\nabla_{x}^{m}\mathsf{H}^{im}[f](t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\lesssim(\|f-\phi\|_{X_{T}}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}\mathfrak{M}_{\phi})^{2}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}})^{2(m+10)}.

(4.44)

Proof.
Recall that

\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]=-\frac{1}{\mu_{+}}e_{d+1}\cdot\bigl(\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\mathcal{Q}[f]\bigr).

Thus, by the product estimates in Hölder and Besov spaces, for
j=0,m
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{j}\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}}\lesssim\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=j}\left(\|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{C}^{j_{1}}}\|\nabla_{x}^{j_{2}}\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}}+\|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{C}^{j_{1}+\kappa-2}}\|\nabla_{x}^{j_{2}}\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}}\right),

and similarly,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{j}\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}}\lesssim\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=j}\left(\|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{C}^{j_{1}}}\|\nabla_{x}^{j_{2}}\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}}+\|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{j_{1}+\kappa-2}}\|\nabla_{x}^{j_{2}}\mathcal{Q}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}}\right).

Applying Lemma
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{C}^{j_{1}}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{j_{1}}{3}}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{j_{1}+2},

\displaystyle|\log t|^{\varkappa}\|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{C}^{j_{1}+\kappa-2}}+\|\mathsf{M}(\nabla f)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{j_{1}+\kappa-2}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{j_{1}+\kappa-2}{3}}\|f\|_{T,*}(1+\|f\|_{T})^{j_{1}+2}.

Combining this with the estimates of
\mathcal{Q}[f]
in Lemma
4.12
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{j+\kappa}{3}}

\displaystyle(|\log t|^{\varkappa}\|\nabla_{x}^{j}\mathsf{H}^{im}[f](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}}+\|\nabla_{x}^{j}\mathsf{H}^{im}[f](t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\lesssim(\|f-\phi\|_{X_{T}}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}\mathfrak{M}_{\phi})^{2}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}})^{2(m+10)}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

4.3
Contraction estimates

We now establish contraction estimates for the nonlinear terms in (
3.29
). These estimates are used in the uniqueness argument and in the stability of the approximation scheme. The explicit term
\mathsf{H}^{ex}
is handled by the same commutator estimates as in Lemma
4.3
, while the implicit term
\mathsf{H}^{im}
requires comparing the corresponding elliptic transmission problems.

Lemma 4.14

Let
\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f]
be as defined in (
3.30
), the norm
\|\cdot\|_{T}
be as defined in (
4.2
) with constants
\varkappa,m,\kappa
fixed in (
4.1
). Then for any
T\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, there holds

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}

\displaystyle|\log t|^{\varkappa}(t^{\frac{\kappa}{3}}\|(\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f]-\mathsf{H}^{ex}[g])(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}\|\nabla^{m}(\mathsf{H}^{ex}[f]-\mathsf{H}^{ex}[g])(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{\kappa-2}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\|f-g\|_{T}(\|(f-\phi,g-\phi)\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}\mathfrak{M}_{\phi})(1+\|(f,g,\phi)\|_{T})^{2m+5},

where
\mathfrak{M}_{\phi}=\|\phi\|_{H^{m+4}\cap C^{m+4}}
.

The proof is identical to that of Lemma
4.3
, with one factor replaced by the difference
f-g
.

Now we consider the implicit term
\mathsf{H}^{im}[f]-\mathsf{H}^{im}[g]
. As preparation, we first renormalize the
transmission elliptic system. Suppose that
f,g
satisfy (
4.13
), and
v^{\pm}_{f},v^{\pm}_{g}
are solutions to (
3.3
) with interfaces
f
and
g
, respectively. Define
a^{\pm}(x)=\frac{\underline{b}^{\pm}(x)-f(x)}{\underline{b}^{\pm}(x)-g(x)}
and
\bar{v}^{\pm}_{g}(x,z)=v^{\pm}_{g}(x,a^{\pm}(x)z)
, which are defined in the same domain as
v_{f}^{\pm}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{x,z}(\tilde{\mathsf{M}}^{\pm}(x,z)\nabla_{x,z}\bar{v}_{g}^{\pm})=0,\ \ \text{in}\ \tilde{\Omega}_{f}^{\pm},

(4.45)

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{g}^{-}-\bar{v}_{g}^{+}=\mathcal{G}[g]+\varrho_{0}g,\quad\text{ on }\{z=0\},

\displaystyle e_{d+1}\cdot((\mu_{+}^{-1}\tilde{\mathsf{M}}^{+}(x,z)\nabla_{x,z}\bar{v}_{g}^{+}-\mu_{-}^{-1}\tilde{\mathsf{M}}^{-}(x,z)\nabla_{x,z}\bar{v}_{g}^{-}))=0,\quad\text{ on }\{z=0\},

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{f}^{\pm}\cdot(\tilde{\mathsf{M}}^{\pm}(x,z)\nabla_{x,z}\bar{v}_{g}^{\pm})=0,\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{on}\ \tilde{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{f},

where
